# Yunus ministry

This article is about the people in Muhammad Yunus-led government. For the events of his tenure as chief adviser, see [Interim government of Muhammad Yunus](77721275.md).

| Yunus ministry |  |
| --- | --- |

| 8 August 2024 – 17 February 2026 |  |

An [interim government](77721275.md) led by [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md) was formed on 8 August 2024 in [Bangladesh](3454.md), following the [Resignation of Sheikh Hasina](77539154.md) on 5 August 2024 amid [nationwide protests against the government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_uprising). Following the dissolution of the [12th Jatiya Sangsad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12th_Jatiya_Sangsad) on 6 August 2024, the [interim cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interim_government_\(Bangladesh\)) was in office until a new Prime Minister was appointed after a [snap](462915.md) [general election](72614737.md). The government, like the previous non-[caretaker government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caretaker_government_of_Bangladesh) interim administrations (in [1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayem_ministry) and [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahabuddin_Ahmed_ministry)), is extra-constitutional. However, [Appellate Division, Supreme Court of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appellate_Division,_Supreme_Court_of_Bangladesh) affirmed the legality of the stopgap government on 9 August 2024, stating that the [doctrine of necessity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctrine_of_necessity) created an urgent need to manage state affairs and address the constitutional vacuum,[^cite_note-Muhammad_Yunus-led_interim_govt_legal:_SC-1][^cite_note-\:0-2][^cite_note-\:37-3] similar to previous cases. The main pledge of his ministry was to forge consensus about and implement fundamental reforms that are required to hold a free and fair [general election](72614737.md) and [constitutional referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Bangladeshi_constitutional_referendum) on the [July Charter](80557195.md) alongside the election on 12 February 2026.[^cite_note-4]

President [Mohammed Shahabuddin](73010562.md) administered the oath of office to Yunus and his council of advisers at [Bangabhaban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangabhaban) on 8 August 2024.[^cite_note-The_Daily_Star_Bangladesh-5] The cabinet consists of 1 [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)), 20 [Advisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adviser_\(Interim_government_of_Bangladesh\)), 8 Special positions under the Chief Adviser's office and 5 Special assistants to the Chief Adviser under the [Minister of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_State). The [Jamuna State Guest House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamuna_State_Guest_House) was served as the official residence of the Chief Adviser.[^cite_note-The_Daily_Star_Bangladesh-5][^cite_note-\:55-6]

## Background

Main article: [July Uprising](78109234.md)

Further information: [2024 Bangladesh quota reform movement](77355536.md) and [Non-cooperation movement (2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-cooperation_movement_\(2024\))

On 5 August 2024, [Prime Minister of Bangladesh](461887.md) [Sheikh Hasina](623439.md) [resigned and went on an exile to India](77539154.md) amidst [a nationwide uprising](78109234.md). Initially focused on issues such as quota reforms, the movement gradually intensified following reports of [violence and mass casualties](77480962.md). This was later referred to as the July Uprising, culminated in calls for Sheikh Hasina and her cabinet to step down. The movement was widely perceived as [pro-democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratization) in nature.

Following her resignation, [Chief of Army Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Army_Staff_\(Bangladesh\)) General [Waker-uz-Zaman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waker-uz-Zaman) and President [Mohammed Shahabuddin](73010562.md) announced the formation of an interim government. The president initiated discussions with political leaders across the country and dissolved [the parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12th_Jatiya_Sangsad) on 6 August 2024. The [Students Against Discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_Against_Discrimination), lead organisation of the uprising, proposed economist and [Nobel laureate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_laureate) [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md), who was then residing in Paris, to head the interim government. Yunus accepted the proposal, and the interim government was finalized on 7 August 2024.

## Dissolution



Muhammad Yunus giving his farewell speech as the Chief Advisor of Bangladesh on 16 February 2026.

On 17 February 2026, followed by the landslide victory of the [Bangladesh Nationalist Party](309760.md) (BNP) lead alliance in the 12th national election, [Tarique Rahman](5601422.md) took oath as Prime Minister along with a new cabinet marking the end of the [Interim government of Muhammad Yunus](77721275.md).[^cite_note-\:1-7][^cite_note-\:2-8][^cite_note-9]

## Formation and timeline

Note: All information regarding adviser appointments and ministry portfolios is based on publicly issued notifications by the Cabinet Division, Government of the People's Republic of Bangladesh.

### 8 August 2024

- [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md) was appointed by the President [Mohammed Shahabuddin](73010562.md) as the [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)) to the [interim government of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interim_government_of_Bangladesh).

  [^cite_note-10]

- 16 individuals were also appointed as [advisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)) to the interim government by the [President](404877.md):

  [^cite_note-11]

  1. [Salehuddin Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salehuddin_Ahmed_\(economist\))

  2. [Asif Nazrul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asif_Nazrul)

  3. [Adilur Rahman Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adilur_Rahman_Khan)

  4. [A. F. Hassan Ariff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._F._Hassan_Ariff)

  5. [Md. Touhid Hossain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Md._Touhid_Hossain)

  6. [Rizwana Hasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rizwana_Hasan)

  7. [Sharmeen Murshid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharmeen_Murshid)

  8. [Faruk-e-Azam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faruk-e-Azam)

  9. [M Sakhawat Hussain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M_Sakhawat_Hussain)

  10. [Supradip Chakma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supradip_Chakma)

  11. [Bidhan Ranjan Roy Poddar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidhan_Ranjan_Roy_Poddar)

  12. [A F M Khalid Hossain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_F_M_Khalid_Hossain)

  13. [Farida Akhter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farida_Akhter)

  14. [Nurjahan Begum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nurjahan_Begum_\(banker\))

  15. [Nahid Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahid_Islam)

  16. [Asif Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asif_Mahmud)

- Yunus was sworn in by the [President](404877.md) at the [Bangabhaban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangabhaban).

  [^cite_note-12]

  &#x20;Along with him, 13 of the 16 appointed advisers also took the oath, also administered by the [President](404877.md). It was announced that the remaining three advisers—[Supradip Chakma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supradip_Chakma), [Bidhan Ranjan Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidhan_Ranjan_Roy), and [Faruk-e-Azam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faruk-e-Azam) would take their oaths at a later time.

  [^cite_note-13]

  &#x20;The oath ceremony was attended by national and international guests, including several ambassadors and the chiefs of the [Bangladesh Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Armed_Forces)—the [Chief of Army Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Army_Staff_\(Bangladesh\)), the [Chief of Naval Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Naval_Staff_\(Bangladesh\)), and the [Chief of Air Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Air_Staff_\(Bangladesh\)). Senior political figures from various political parties (except the [Awami League](404861.md)) were also present, alongside many other distinguished guests.

  [^cite_note-14]

### 9 August 2024

- The distribution of [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions was made public. A total of 27 ministries and divisions were assigned to [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)) [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md). One ministry was assigned to each of the other 13 advisers.

### 11 August 2024

- [Supradip Chakma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supradip_Chakma) and [Bidhan Ranjan Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidhan_Ranjan_Roy) were sworn in as advisers by the President.

  [^cite_note-15]

  [^cite_note-16]

- Following their appointment, the responsibility for [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions was redistributed among [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md), [Supradip Chakma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supradip_Chakma) and [Bidhan Ranjan Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidhan_Ranjan_Roy). One [ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) was allocated to each of the two advisers, resulting in 25 [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions being managed by the [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)).

### 13 August 2024

- [Faruk-e-Azam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faruk-e-Azam), was sworn in as adviser.

  [^cite_note-17]

  [^cite_note-18]

- Following his appointment, a redistribution of [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions was carried out between [Yunus](548105.md) and [Faruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faruk-e-Azam). [Faruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faruk-e-Azam) was assigned a ministry, resulting in 24 [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions being managed by the [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)).

  [^cite_note-19]

- [Shafiqul Alam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shafiqul_Alam), a seasoned journalist and Bangladesh Bureau Chief of [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) was appointed as [Press Secretary to the Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser's_Office_\(Bangladesh\)) with status of a Secretary.

### 14 August 2024

- [Lamiya Morshed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamiya_Morshed), executive director of [Yunus Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yunus_Centre) and Grameen Healthcare Trust, was appointed as [Principal Coordinator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser's_Office_\(Bangladesh\)) for [Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals) Affairs with the status of a Senior Secretary.

### 16 August 2024

- Additional four Advisers—[Wahiduddin Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wahiduddin_Mahmud), [Ali Imam Majumder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Imam_Majumder), [Muhammad Fouzul Kabir Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Fouzul_Kabir_Khan), and [Jahangir Alam Chowdhury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jahangir_Alam_Chowdhury) were appointed by President [Mohammed Shahabuddin](73010562.md) and were sworn in on the same day.

  [^cite_note-20]

  [^cite_note-21]

- Following their appointment, a redistribution of [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions was carried out between the [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)) and all the Advisers. A total of 10 [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions were taken charge of by the [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)). Responsibilities for one [ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) each were assigned to many of the other advisers, while some advisers were assigned multiple [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)).

- [Ali Imam Majumder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Imam_Majumder) was appointed as [Special Assistant to the Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Assistant_to_the_Chief_Adviser), a role equivalent to an adviser. In the official document, his role is described as being "Attached to the [Office of the Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser's_Office_\(Bangladesh\))".

### 22 August 2024

- [Abdul Hafiz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Hafiz_\(Lieutenant_General\)) was appointed as [Special Assistant to the Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Assistant_to_the_Chief_Adviser) on Defense and National Integration, a role equivalent to an adviser.

  [^cite_note-23]

  [^cite_note-73]

### 20 April 2025

- [Mohammad Sufiur Rahman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Sufiur_Rahman) appointed as Special Assistant (with the status of [Minister of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_State)) for [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Bangladesh\)).

  [^cite_note-74]

### 14 July 2025

- [Sheikh Moin Uddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Moin_Uddin) was given the responsibility of [Ministry of Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Railways_\(Bangladesh\)) as additional duty.

### 13 November 2025

- [Ali Riaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Riaz) a political scientist and Head of [Constitutional Reform Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Reform_Commission) appointed as [Special Assistant to the Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Assistant_to_the_Chief_Adviser), with the status equivalent to an [Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)).

  [^cite_note-75]

### 10 December 2025

- [Asif Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asif_Mahmud) and [Mahfuj Alam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahfuj_Alam) resigns from the ministry.

  [^cite_note-76]

  [^cite_note-77]

- [Ministry of Youth and Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Youth_and_Sports_\(Bangladesh\)), [Ministry of Local Government, Rural Development and Co-operatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Local_Government,_Rural_Development_and_Co-operatives) and [Ministry of Information and Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_\(Bangladesh\)) the three ministries under Adviser [Asif Mahmud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asif_Mahmud) and [Mahfuj Alam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahfuj_Alam) are shifted to [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)) [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md).

### 11 December 2025

- A redistribution of [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions was carried out between [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)) and three other advisers, resulting in 5 [ministries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_\(government_department\)) and divisions being managed by the [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)).

  [^cite_note-78]

### 24 December 2025

- [Khoda Baksh Chowdhury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khoda_Baksh_Chowdhury) resigned as Special Assistant (with the status of [Minister of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_State)) for [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(Bangladesh\)).

  [^cite_note-79]

### 30 December 2025

- [Md Sayedur Rahman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Md_Sayedur_Rahman), the Special Assistant for the [Ministry of Health and Family Welfare](40314029.md) resigns.

  [^cite_note-80]

### 1 January 2026

- [Md Sayedur Rahman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Md_Sayedur_Rahman), rejoins as Special Assistant for the [Ministry of Health and Family Welfare](40314029.md).

  [^cite_note-81]

### 12 February 2026

- [13th Parliamentary Election](72614737.md) and [National Referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Bangladeshi_constitutional_referendum) is held.

  [^cite_note-82]

### 13 February 2026

- [Election Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Election_Commission) publishes the results of the [13th Parliamentary Election](72614737.md) in a [Bangladesh Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh_Gazette). [Bangladesh Nationalist Party](309760.md) wins 209 seats and moves to form the government. The results of the [National Referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Bangladeshi_constitutional_referendum) are published in a separate gazette, with the “Yes” vote winning.

  [^cite_note-83]

  [^cite_note-84]

  [^cite_note-85]

  [^cite_note-86]

### 16 February 2026

- [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md) [addresses the nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speeches_by_Muhammad_Yunus_as_Chief_Adviser_of_Bangladesh) for the last time as [Chief Adviser of Bangladesh](9219750.md).

  [^cite_note-87]

### 17 February 2026

- [Tarique Rahman](5601422.md) takes oath as the [Prime Minister of Bangladesh](461887.md) marking the end of the [Interim government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interim_government_\(Bangladesh\)) led by [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md).

  [^cite_note-\:1-7]

  [^cite_note-\:2-8]

## Reform commissions

Main article: [Reform Commissions of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Commissions_of_Bangladesh)

The ministry has formed eleven reform commissions to reform perceived important areas of the government. The Head of Commission will have a status of a Justice of [Appellate Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appellate_Division,_Supreme_Court_of_Bangladesh) of the [Supreme Court of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Bangladesh).[^cite_note-119]

The details of the head of commission and members are as follows.[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123]

Note: All information regarding the formation of all the committees is based on publicly issued notifications by the Cabinet Division, Government of the People's Republic of Bangladesh.

## Councils, committees, and commissions

The [Interim government](77721275.md) led by [Muhammad Yunus](548105.md) formed several new councils, committees, and commissions, and reformed existing ones to manage various areas of the government. The councils, committees, and commissions consists of [Chief Adviser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Adviser_\(Bangladesh\)), several advisers and government officials. The details about the committees are as follows.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152][^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158]

Note: All information regarding the formation of all the committees is based on publicly issued notifications by the Cabinet Division, Government of the People's Republic of Bangladesh.

## Constitutional legality



The [Jamuna State Guest House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamuna_State_Guest_House) is currently being used as the office and official residence of the Chief Adviser of the interim government.

In spite of the existence of interim administrations in the 1975–1978 ([Sayem ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayem_ministry)) and 1990–1991 ([Shahabuddin Ahmed ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahabuddin_Ahmed_ministry)) periods of Bangladeshi political history, there were significant concerns regarding the legality of an interim government, considering that the [Constitution of Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Bangladesh) does not allow for it to exist. The Constitution also forbids [ouster clauses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ouster_clause), following the [doctrine of basic constitutional structure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_structure_doctrine).

The only form of interim government that has been constitutional in the past under the country's law was the [Caretaker government system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caretaker_government_of_Bangladesh) introduced in 1996 through the [13th Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amendments_to_the_Constitution_of_Bangladesh#Thirteenth_Amendment) but this was overturned by the Awami League regime in 2011 through the [15th Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amendments_to_the_Constitution_of_Bangladesh#Fifteenth_Amendment), which repealed the former.[^cite_note-188]

However, with [Awami League President](404861.md) and former Prime Minister [Sheikh Hasina](623439.md) resigning and fleeing the country amid protests,[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] the Appellate Division of the Bangladeshi Supreme Court has acknowledged the legitimacy of the Yunus government under the [doctrine of necessity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctrine_of_necessity). The court took the line that Hasina's resignation created a situation for which there was no constitutional remedy. Not only does the Bangladeshi legal system no longer account for transfers of power, but there was an urgent need to oversee state affairs. In essence, the court upheld what essentially amounts to the wording of the 13th Amendment prior to its suspension: "The Appellate Division opined that in accordance with Article 106 of the Constitution, an interim government can be formed with a chief advisor and a few other advisors in the absence of parliament" as per [Chief Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_Bangladesh) [Obaidul Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obaidul_Hassan).[^cite_note-191] Hassan himself a staunch supporter of the ousted Awami League, has since resigned as Chief Justice.[^cite_note-192]

Hence, despite the lack of constitutional legality for an interim government, due to extremely broad popular support and the need to ensure that a [military junta](216815.md) takeover [like in 1975](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3_November_1975_Bangladeshi_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) is prevented, Yunus was sworn in on the night of 8 August 2024.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194] The Yunus government enjoys near-universal recognition from the international community, with many Head of state and Ministers of Foreign Affairs of other nations having congratulated Yunus upon his swearing-in.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197]

On 17 November 2025, the Interim government sentenced Sheikh Hasina to death after being found guilty of committing various crimes against humanity. Hasina condemned the ruling as "biased and politically motivated" and alleged that "the death penalty is the interim government's way of nullifying her party the Awami League as a political force," in a five-page statement released after the verdict.[^cite_note-\:55-6]

## Reactions

### Internal

- The nomination of Yunus, who accepted an offer to advise the interim government, has been supported by key figures in the student movement.

  [^cite_note-198]

  [^cite_note-199]

  &#x20;Students Against Discrimination coordinator [Nahid Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahid_Islam) (who later became an adviser to the interim government) said on 6 August 2024,

  [^cite_note-200]

  > We have decided that an interim government would be formed, in which internationally renowned Nobel laureate Muhammad Yunus, who has wide acceptability, would be the chief adviser.

- Former [Member of Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_\(Bangladesh\)) and business leader [Abdul Kader Azad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Kader_Azad) said in his response,&#x20;

  > The government formed under the leadership of Muhammad Yunus has the support of our businessmen. We hope that the collapsed law and order situation will be restored as soon as possible. All the students and people who were killed will be tried through a special investigation committee and a special court.[^cite_note-201]

- [Jatiya Samajtantrik Dal (Inu)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jatiya_Samajtantrik_Dal_\(Inu\)) president [Hasanul Haq Inu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasanul_Haq_Inu) and general secretary [Shirin Akhter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirin_Akhter) said in a statement:&#x20;

  > As the primary and main task of the Interim government, effective steps must be taken to restore normalcy and peace in public life by ensuring the safety of people's lives and property by re-establishing law and order in the country without delay. After the interim government assumes office, it will be ensured that not a single incident of loss of life, attack and destruction of property occurs in the country.[^cite_note-202]

- Joint General Secretary of the [Awami League](404861.md) [Mahbubul Alam Hanif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahbubul_Alam_Hanif) has congratulated the interim government, which has taken the oath of office after the ouster of the government led by the party, through a Facebook post.

  [^cite_note-203]

  &#x20;He also urged the government to take strict measures to stop killings, looting and arson attacks across the country.

### International

#### Governments

- [Canada](5042916.md) [Minister of Foreign Affairs of Canada](270882.md) [Mélanie Joly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A9lanie_Joly) welcomed the inauguration of the interim government in Bangladesh led by Dr. Yunus.

  [^cite_note-204]

- [China](5405.md) [Lin Jian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Jian), [spokesperson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_spokespersons_of_the_Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_of_China) for the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs of China](7453497.md) said, "China has noted the establishment of an interim government of Bangladesh and welcomes this".

  [^cite_note-205]

- [India](14533.md) [Prime minister of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_minister_of_India) [Narendra Modi](444222.md) congratulated Yunus after he was sworn in as a chief of the interim government. Modi said, "We hope for an early return to normalcy, ensuring the safety and protection of [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Bangladesh) and all other minority communities".

  [^cite_note-206]

  &#x20;[Leader of Opposition of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_\(India\)) [Rahul Gandhi](305010.md) & Chief Minister of [West Bengal](34040.md) [Mamata Banerjee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamata_Banerjee) also congratulated Yunus on being sworn in as the head of Bangladesh's interim government.

  [^cite_note-207]

- [South Korea](27019.md) A spokesperson for the [South Korean Ministry of Foreign Affairs](11533941.md) expressed hope to strengthen [Bangladesh–South Korea relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladesh%E2%80%93South_Korea_relations) with the interim government.

  [^cite_note-208]

- [Japan](15573.md) [Japanese ambassador to Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Japan,_Dhaka), Naoki Ito, congratulated and extended their full support.

  [^cite_note-209]

- [Malaysia](3607937.md) [Prime Minister of Malaysia](496731.md) [Anwar Ibrahim](19264815.md) congratulated Yunus on his appointment as Chief Adviser of the interim government of Bangladesh.

  [^cite_note-210]

- [Maldives](19117.md) [President of Maldives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Maldives) [Mohamed Muizzu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Muizzu) congratulated Yunus on his appointment as the Chief Adviser of the interim government of the People's Republic of Bangladesh.

  [^cite_note-211]

- [Sri Lanka](26750.md) [President of Sri Lanka](359933.md) [Ranil Wickremesinghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranil_Wickremesinghe) congratulates Yunus and his interim Government of Bangladesh.

  [^cite_note-212]

- [Netherlands](21148.md) The [Dutch Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Netherlands\)) welcomed the interim government of Dr. Yunus.

  [^cite_note-213]

  &#x20;Prime Minister [Dick Schoof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Schoof) also congratulated Dr. Yunus, stating, "Our countries enjoy long-standing and cordial ties. I look forward to supporting your interim government and further deepening our cooperation."

  [^cite_note-214]

- [Pakistan](23235.md) [Prime minister of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_minister_of_Pakistan) [Shehbaz Sharif](6502561.md) wished Yunus great success in guiding Bangladesh towards a harmonious and prosperous future. He looks forward to working with him to deepen cooperation between Pakistan and Bangladesh.

  [^cite_note-215]

  &#x20;Many leaders of Opposition Party [Pakistan Tehreek-e-Insaf](1651925.md) also congratulates Interim government of Bangladesh.

- [Turkey](11125639.md) President of Turkiye [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) congratulated Yunus.

  [^cite_note-216]

  &#x20;Erdoğan described Yunus as "a friend of Islam and Turkey", adding that "Turkey will continue to provide support in this turbulent time".

  [^cite_note-217]

- [United States](3434750.md) [Spokesperson for the United States Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spokesperson_for_the_United_States_Department_of_State) [Matthew Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Miller_\(spokesperson\)) told reporters: "We welcome Dr. Yunus's call for an end to the recent violence and we stand ready to work with the interim government and Dr. Yunus as it charts a democratic future for the people of Bangladesh."

  [^cite_note-aljaz-218]

- [France](5843419.md) [President of France](24899.md) [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) congratulated Dr Yunus & extended full support.

  [^cite_note-219]

- [United Kingdom](31717.md) [Prime Minister of the United Kingdom](24150.md) [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) welcomed the Interim Government of the Bangladesh and pledged support.

  [^cite_note-220]

#### Organizations

- [High Representative of the Union for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy](750942.md) [Josep Borrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep_Borrell) said, "The EU looks forward to engaging with the new administration and to supporting this critical transition which should be part of a peaceful and inclusive process underpinned by good governance, democratic values and respect for human rights".

  [^cite_note-221]

- An alliance of 255 revolutionary groups and civil society organizations in [Myanmar](19457.md) published an open letter congratulating the establishment of the interim government and appealed to Yunus to collaborate with and support the [National Unity Government of Myanmar](67413816.md) against the [State Administration Council](66589972.md).

  [^cite_note-222]

  [^cite_note-223]
